## Step 1: Embed 1, Structured data (JSON-LD)

File: `00-mastt-aup-schema.html`. Add an HTML Embed at the top of the post body and paste this as it is. (4,602 characters).

```html
<script type="application/ld+json">
{
  "@context": "https://schema.org",
  "@graph": [
    {
      "@type": "Article",
      "headline": "Australian Public Construction Procurement: 2026 Statistics",
      "description": "How Australian governments buy and manage construction, from federal contract thresholds and amendments to council tenders, payment times and defect orders.",
      "articleSection": "Contract Administration",
      "publisher": {
        "@type": "Organization",
        "name": "Mastt",
        "url": "https://www.mastt.com",
        "logo": {
          "@type": "ImageObject",
          "url": "https://cdn.prod.website-files.com/607f739c92f9cfa16116b34d/607f978bbd76e475e2229a43_Mastt%20Logo.svg"
        }
      },
      "mainEntityOfPage": "https://www.mastt.com/research/australian-public-construction-procurement-statistics-2026"
    },
    {
      "@type": "Dataset",
      "name": "Australian Public Construction Procurement: 2026 Statistics",
      "description": "Records. This is an internal Mastt study of published public records. It draws on: About 31,800 Commonwealth contract notices for building, construction and related services (mostly 2013 to 2026). About 68,000 Queensland contract disclosure rows (mostly 2019 to 2026). Project listings in Queensland state budgets (12 budgets from 2014-15 to 2026-27) and Victorian state budgets (three machine-readable years). 702 Brisbane City Council contract decisions (August 2020 to April 2025). 13,262 ACT invoices (2017 to 2026). 137 NSW building work orders (to 28 September 2026).",
      "creator": {
        "@type": "Organization",
        "name": "Mastt",
        "url": "https://www.mastt.com"
      }
    },
    {
      "@type": "FAQPage",
      "mainEntity": [
        {
          "@type": "Question",
          "name": "How many Commonwealth construction contracts are amended?",
          "acceptedAnswer": {
            "@type": "Answer",
            "text": "33.6% were amended at least once (10,661 of 31,773 contracts, mostly 2013 to 2026). The rate runs from 14.9% under $100,000 to 72.6% over $100 million, and amendments include option exercises and time extensions."
          }
        },
        {
          "@type": "Question",
          "name": "What is the Commonwealth procurement threshold for construction?",
          "acceptedAnswer": {
            "@type": "Answer",
            "text": "The general threshold for non-corporate Commonwealth entities rose from $80,000 to $125,000 on 17 November 2025, including GST. A separate threshold of $7.5 million applies to construction services and was left unchanged."
          }
        },
        {
          "@type": "Question",
          "name": "How often does Brisbane pick the cheapest tender?",
          "acceptedAnswer": {
            "@type": "Answer",
            "text": "The cheapest tender won 59.5% of the 467 priced tenders analysed, so 40.5% went to someone else. The winner was usually within 25% of the cheapest price."
          }
        },
        {
          "@type": "Question",
          "name": "How long does the ACT take to pay construction invoices?",
          "acceptedAnswer": {
            "@type": "Answer",
            "text": "The median was 7 calendar days, or 5 business days, across 13,262 invoices from 2017 to 2026. 9.8% took longer than 20 days and 3.4% longer than 30 days."
          }
        },
        {
          "@type": "Question",
          "name": "How much do Queensland capital projects grow in cost?",
          "acceptedAnswer": {
            "@type": "Answer",
            "text": "Projects listed in three or more state budgets grew a median 24.2% from first to last estimate, and 58.7% grew by more than 10%. Across projects in two or more budgets, 38.1% grew by more than 10%."
          }
        },
        {
          "@type": "Question",
          "name": "How many standard letters does NSW GC21 include?",
          "acceptedAnswer": {
            "@type": "Answer",
            "text": "109 sample letters sit in the GC21 Edition 2 suite, which NSW describes as over 100. The MW21 contract for smaller works has 105 templates."
          }
        },
        {
          "@type": "Question",
          "name": "Are NSW building defect orders rising?",
          "acceptedAnswer": {
            "@type": "Answer",
            "text": "49 orders issued in 2026 to 28 September are listed, against 15 from 2023. The register lists only orders still in force, so earlier years are undercounted and the rise is overstated."
          }
        }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
</script>
```

## Step 2: Text block 1

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

This page is a citable reference to how Australian governments buy and manage construction. It brings together Mastt's analysis of public contract records, from Commonwealth contract notices to council tender decisions, with context from official sources.

 Every figure is labelled with its period. Where a number is our own calculation, we say so, and every outside source is linked where it is used.

 Findings are signals, not proof. Texas figures describe Texas DOT work only. Please cite as: Mastt, Australian Public Construction Procurement: 2026 Statistics, mastt.com.

var mxGen=0;
var mxZero=function(el){if(!el.hasAttribute('data-final'))el.setAttribute('data-final',el.textContent);var dec=+(el.getAttribute('data-dec')||0);el.textContent=(el.getAttribute('data-pre')||'')+(0).toFixed(dec)+(el.getAttribute('data-suf')||'');};
var mxCount=function(el,g){var to=parseFloat(el.getAttribute('data-to'));if(isNaN(to))return;var fin=el.getAttribute('data-final')||el.textContent,dec=+(el.getAttribute('data-dec')||0),pre=el.getAttribute('data-pre')||'',suf=el.getAttribute('data-suf')||'',f=new Intl.NumberFormat('en-US',{minimumFractionDigits:dec,maximumFractionDigits:dec}),t0=null,d=950;el.setAttribute('data-final',fin);var step=function(t){if(g!==mxGen)return;if(t0===null)t0=t;var p=Math.min(1,(t-t0)/d),e=1-Math.pow(1-p,3);el.textContent=pre+f.format(to*e)+suf;if(p<1)requestAnimationFrame(step);else el.textContent=fin;};requestAnimationFrame(step);};

var mxOut=function(){if(!root.classList.contains('maupkeyfindings-in'))return;mxGen++;root.classList.remove('maupkeyfindings-in');root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(mxZero);};

## Step 4: Text block 2

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

## TL;DR

- Commonwealth contracts bunch under the old $80,000 limit: **44% more** than expected sit between $75,000 and $79,999, and **98** sit at exactly $88,000, which is $80,000 plus GST. The limit rose to $125,000 on 17 November 2025 and the pile has not clearly moved yet.
- **33.6%** of Commonwealth construction contracts are amended, from 15% under $100,000 to 73% over $100 million. Defence holds **69%** of the amended contracts.
- In Queensland, **34%** of works worth $200,000 to $1 million went to limited tender and 16% to open tender. Among contracts that state a form, **33%** use the Main Roads contract and 3% use AS 4000.
- Queensland projects listed in three or more budgets grew a median **24%**. In Victoria, **47%** of projects pushed back their finish date, typically by 4 quarters.
- Brisbane gave **40.5%** of priced tenders to someone other than the cheapest tenderer, **23%** of tenders drew a single bid and **12%** of awards skipped tender.
- The ACT pays construction invoices in a median **7 days**, and **75%** of payments land on a Tuesday or Thursday.
- NSW lists **49** building work orders issued in 2026 to 28 September, and its GC21 contract comes with **109** standard letters.

 
## Key statistics at a glance

 **Note on definitions.** A Commonwealth construction contract here means a contract for building, construction or related services, or one with a works-type description. Values are original contract values including GST unless stated. Percentages are shares of records that carry the field in question.

 
### Commonwealth contracts

 - **44% more contracts just under $80,000** than nearby bands predict: 550 Commonwealth contracts valued $75,000 to $79,999 against about 383 expected, mostly 2013 to 2026 (Mastt analysis). The general threshold [rose to $125,000](https://hallandwilcox.com.au/news/commonwealth-procurement-rules-key-updates-to-be-aware-of/) from 17 November 2025.
- **98 contracts at exactly $88,000**, against 24 at exactly $80,000. Twelve agencies account for all 98, and two of them for 71.
- **33.6% of Commonwealth construction contracts were amended**: 10,661 of 31,773 (Mastt analysis, mostly 2013 to 2026).
- **73% of contracts over $100 million were amended**, against 15% of contracts under $100,000.
- **29% median value growth** among amended Commonwealth contracts, or 24% with Defence removed.
- **69% of amended Commonwealth contracts are Defence**, which holds 53% of all contracts.

 
### Queensland and Victoria

 - **34% of Queensland works worth $200,000 to $1 million went to limited tender** and 16% to open tender, with 30% not stating a method (68,394 contracts, mostly 2019 to 2026).
- **33% of Queensland contracts that state a form use the Main Roads Transport Infrastructure Contract**. AS 4000 accounts for 3.1%. Only 450 of 14,804 contracts state a form at all.
- **+24% median cost growth** for Queensland projects listed in three or more state budgets, and 59% grew by more than 10%.
- **47% of Victorian projects** in two or more budgets pushed back their finish date, by a typical 4 quarters.

 
### Brisbane and the ACT

 - **40.5% of priced Brisbane tenders** went to someone other than the cheapest tenderer. Another **23.3%** of tendered awards recorded a single tenderer and **12.0%** of all awards skipped tender.
- **56% of Brisbane tendered awards were lump sum**, 44% schedule of rates.
- **7 days median** for the ACT to pay a construction invoice, with 3.4% taking more than 30 days.
- **75% of ACT payments** to construction suppliers land on a Tuesday or Thursday.

 
### NSW and US comparisons

 - **49 NSW building work orders** issued in 2026 to 28 September are listed, against 15 from 2023. Only orders still in force stay listed.
- **109 standard letters** in the NSW GC21 sample letter suite, 105 in MW21, and 60 clause-numbered notices in the ACT design services suite.
- **98.9% against 59.5%** of awards went to the lowest bid in Texas DOT contracts and Brisbane tenders.
- **62% against 53%** of federal construction contracts come from Defense in the United States and Defence in Australia.

 
## Why do Commonwealth contracts pile up just under $80,000?

 Commonwealth buyers must follow additional procurement rules once a purchase reaches a set value. For most purchases by non-corporate entities that value was $80,000. [Hall & Wilcox](https://hallandwilcox.com.au/news/commonwealth-procurement-rules-key-updates-to-be-aware-of/) reports that it rose to $125,000 under paragraph 9.7 of the [Commonwealth Procurement Rules](https://www.legislation.gov.au/F2025L01263/asmade/text), with effect from 17 November 2025, and that all thresholds in the Rules count GST.

 
### Contracts bunch just under $80,000

 Mastt analysed 31,773 Commonwealth construction contracts with a stated value, signed between 2005 and 2026, 98% of them from 2013. The $75,000 to $79,999 band stands out (Figure 1):

 - **Actual count:** The band holds 550 contracts.
- **Expected count:** A smooth curve fitted to the nearby bands predicts about 383.
- **Excess:** The band holds 44% more contracts than expected.
- **After $80,000:** The count steps down.

var set=function(i){if(i===cur)return;cur=i;cols.forEach(function(el,j){el.classList.toggle('maupf01-on',j===i);});plot.classList.toggle('maupf01-act',i>=0);if(i<0){k.textContent=c.getAttribute('data-avg-label');n.textContent=c.getAttribute('data-avg');w.textContent=c.getAttribute('data-period');}else{var el=cols[i];k.textContent=c.getAttribute('data-val-label');n.textContent=el.getAttribute('data-v');w.textContent=el.getAttribute('data-l');}};
cols.forEach(function(el,i){el.addEventListener('pointerenter',function(){set(i);});el.addEventListener('pointerdown',function(){set(i);});});
plot.addEventListener('pointerleave',function(){set(-1);});plot.addEventListener('blur',function(){set(-1);});
plot.addEventListener('keydown',function(e){if(e.key==='ArrowRight'||e.key==='ArrowLeft'){e.preventDefault();var i=cur<0?(e.key==='ArrowRight'?0:cols.length-1):cur+(e.key==='ArrowRight'?1:-1);set(Math.max(0,Math.min(cols.length-1,i)));}else if(e.key==='Escape'){set(-1);}});});

## Step 7: Text block 3

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

### The spike at $88,000

 The tallest bar sits in the $88,000 band, which holds 159 contracts against 70 in the band below and 58 in the band above. Most of that spike is 98 contracts valued at exactly $88,000, which is $80,000 plus 10% GST.

 Because the Rules count GST, a contract at $88,000 is above the old $80,000 limit. The pattern suggests some buyers read the limit as excluding GST.

 The spike is narrow. It reads as a habit among a few buyers, not a national convention:

 - **Twelve agencies:** They account for all 98 contracts at $88,000.
- **Two agencies:** They account for 71 of the 98.
- **Project fees:** 38 of the 98 are described as project management or project fees.

 Round figures plus GST are common across the range: $66,000, $77,000 and $110,000 all rank among the ten most common exact values, and each equals a round amount plus 10% GST (Figure 2).

## Step 9: Text block 4

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

### Why the pile sits at $80,000 and not at $7.5 million

 The Rules set a separate threshold of $7.5 million for construction services, and Hall & Wilcox reports that it was left unchanged. The pile near $80,000 therefore points to the general threshold applying to much of the building-related work agencies buy, such as maintenance, project management and design, rather than to works contracts as such.

 Bunching below procurement thresholds is a documented pattern. [Palguta and Pertold (2017)](https://researchportal.uc3m.es/display/act549288) found that public buyers clustered contracts just below newly introduced discretionary thresholds, mostly in construction and services.

 Bunching does not prove improper conduct. Lighter rules below a threshold can also save time and cost on small purchases.

 
### Is the pile about to move?

 The change applies to new approaches to market released on or after 17 November 2025, according to [MinterEllison](https://www.minterellison.com/articles/new-commonwealth-procurement-rules-commence-17-november-2025). Contracts signed in the months after that date mostly came from approaches released before it, so any effect arrives slowly.

 Of 1,915 contracts signed from 17 November 2025 to 28 September 2026:

 - **$75,000 to $79,999:** 33 contracts, where about 21 would be expected.
- **$120,000 to $124,999:** 17 contracts, where about 15 would be expected.

 That is no clear move yet. The number to watch is the count just under $125,000 in contracts signed from late 2026.

 **What this means for project teams:** Value contracts on the same GST basis the Rules use, and record how each scope was sized against a threshold. Suppliers should expect small-works and services scopes sized to fit under the limit, and pipeline managers should watch for the pile moving to $125,000.

 
## How often are Commonwealth construction contracts amended?

 Of 31,773 Commonwealth construction contracts, 10,661 (33.6%) were amended at least once, mostly 2013 to 2026. The rate rises with contract size (Figure 3):

 - **Under $100,000:** About 1 in 7 contracts was amended.
- **$1 million and above:** Roughly 7 in 10 contracts were amended.

---

## Step 11: Text block 5

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

### Amendments are not all scope change

 The Commonwealth contract notice system records option exercises and time extensions as amendments, so the rate overstates how often scope changed. Among amended contracts, the median value growth was 29.4% (10,302 contracts).

 
### Small contracts grow more in proportion

 Smaller contracts that are amended grow by more in proportion (Figure 4):

 - **Under $100,000:** The median amended contract grew 79.6%.
- **Over $100 million:** The median amended contract grew 7.3%.

 One extra variation weighs more on a small base.

---

## Step 13: Text block 6

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

### How much of this is Defence?

 Defence holds 53.0% of the contracts and 68.8% of the amended ones (Figure 5). Separating Defence changes two measures:

 - **Amendment rate:** 43.5% for Defence, against 22.3% for every other agency combined.
- **Value growth:** With Defence removed, amended contracts grew a median 24.4%, against 29.4% overall.

 Any national benchmark for Commonwealth construction is largely a Defence benchmark, so split it out before comparing an agency's own rate.

<script>(function(){var root=document.getElementById('mastt-aup-f05');if(!root||root.getAttribute('data-maupf05-on'))return;root.setAttribute('data-maupf05-on','1');
var RM=!root.classList.contains('maupf05-js');
var mxGen=0;
var mxZero=function(el){if(!el.hasAttribute('data-final'))el.setAttribute('data-final',el.textContent);var dec=+(el.getAttribute('data-dec')||0);el.textContent=(el.getAttribute('data-pre')||'')+(0).toFixed(dec)+(el.getAttribute('data-suf')||'');};
var mxCount=function(el,g){var to=parseFloat(el.getAttribute('data-to'));if(isNaN(to))return;var fin=el.getAttribute('data-final')||el.textContent,dec=+(el.getAttribute('data-dec')||0),pre=el.getAttribute('data-pre')||'',suf=el.getAttribute('data-suf')||'',f=new Intl.NumberFormat('en-US',{minimumFractionDigits:dec,maximumFractionDigits:dec}),t0=null,d=950;el.setAttribute('data-final',fin);var step=function(t){if(g!==mxGen)return;if(t0===null)t0=t;var p=Math.min(1,(t-t0)/d),e=1-Math.pow(1-p,3);el.textContent=pre+f.format(to*e)+suf;if(p<1)requestAnimationFrame(step);else el.textContent=fin;};requestAnimationFrame(step);};
var mxIn=function(){if(root.classList.contains('maupf05-in'))return;mxGen++;var g=mxGen;root.classList.add('maupf05-in');root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(function(el){mxCount(el,g);});};
var mxOut=function(){if(!root.classList.contains('maupf05-in'))return;mxGen++;root.classList.remove('maupf05-in');root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(mxZero);};
if(RM||!('IntersectionObserver' in window)){root.classList.add('maupf05-in');}else{root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(mxZero);var io=new IntersectionObserver(function(es){es.forEach(function(e){if(e.isIntersecting&&e.intersectionRatio>=.15){mxIn();}else if(!e.isIntersecting){mxOut();}});},{threshold:[0,.15]});io.observe(root);}
root.querySelectorAll('.maupf05-tabs').forEach(function(box){var tabs=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.maupf05-tablist>.maupf05-tab')),panels=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.maupf05-panel'));var pick=function(v,focus){tabs.forEach(function(t){var on=t.getAttribute('data-v')===v;t.setAttribute('aria-selected',on?'true':'false');t.tabIndex=on?0:-1;if(on&&focus)t.focus();});panels.forEach(function(p){p.hidden=p.getAttribute('data-v')!==v;});box.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('maupf05-tab',{bubbles:true}));};tabs.forEach(function(t,i){t.addEventListener('click',function(){pick(t.getAttribute('data-v'));});t.addEventListener('keydown',function(e){var k=e.key,j=k==='ArrowRight'?i+1:k==='ArrowLeft'?i-1:-1;if(j<0&&k!=='ArrowLeft')return;e.preventDefault();var n=tabs[(j+tabs.length)%tabs.length];pick(n.getAttribute('data-v'),true);});});});
var mxTip=function(host){var tip=host.querySelector(':scope>.maupf05-tip');if(!tip){tip=document.createElement('div');tip.className='maupf05-tip';tip.setAttribute('aria-hidden','true');host.appendChild(tip);}return{show:function(x,y,h){tip.innerHTML=h;tip.style.left=x+'px';tip.style.top=y+'px';tip.classList.add('maupf05-on');},hide:function(){tip.classList.remove('maupf05-on');}};};
try{
(function(){
var GAP=0.5,pf=new Intl.NumberFormat('en-US',{style:'percent',maximumFractionDigits:0}),
share=function(s){return s>0&&s<.01?'<1%':pf.format(s);},
num=/^([^\d-]*)(-?[\d,]*\.?\d+)(.*)$/,

## Step 15: Text block 7

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

**What this means for project teams:** Plan for change as the normal case on anything at $1 million and above, where 7 in 10 contracts are amended. Report option exercises and time extensions separately from scope change, and if you are a non-Defence agency, benchmark against the non-Defence rate of 22%.

 
## How do Queensland agencies buy construction?

 Queensland agencies must publish details of awarded contracts of $10,000 and above, including GST, within 60 days of award, according to the [Queensland Government contract disclosure guide](https://www.forgov.qld.gov.au/__data/assets/pdf_file/0021/183630/Contract-disclosure-guide.pdf). For contracts of $500,000 and above, the guide requires the procurement method to be recorded as open, selective or limited.

 That requirement may help explain why the share of disclosed contracts with no method recorded falls as value rises:

 - **Under $200,000:** No method is recorded for 73% of disclosed contracts.
- **$200,000 to $1 million:** No method is recorded for 30%.
- **$1 million to $10 million:** No method is recorded for 3%.

 
### Disclosure is imperfect

 The [Queensland Audit Office](https://www.qao.qld.gov.au/sites/default/files/reports/confidentiality_and_disclosure_of_government_contracts_report_8-2017-18.pdf) examined 90 contracts across five departments and found:

 - **Appropriately disclosed:** Only 25% of the contracts.
- **Not disclosed:** 21% were not disclosed at all.

 The figures below are best read as a floor for what agencies did, not a full record.

 
### Open tender takes over as contracts grow

 Mastt analysed 68,394 Queensland works-related contracts from about 70 agencies, mostly awarded from 2019 to 2026 and worth A$145 billion in total. Procurement method varies with contract value (Figure 6):

 - **$200,000 to $1 million:** 34% went to limited tender and 16% to open tender, with 15% selective and 30% not stated.
- **$1 million to $10 million:** 31% were limited and 46% open.
- **Above $10 million:** Open tender covers 74%.
- **By value:** Open tender accounts for 48% of the total and limited tender for 36%.

## Step 17: Text block 8

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

### Which contract forms does Queensland use?

 Only 450 of 14,804 Queensland contracts (3%) state their form of contract. Among those that do, the Main Roads Transport Infrastructure Contract leads (Figure 7):

 - **Main Roads contract:** 33.3% use the Main Roads Transport Infrastructure Contract.
- **Other forms:** 22.2%.
- **Purchase orders:** 14.7%.
- **Stand-alone or service agreements:** 12.4%.
- **Panel or standing offers:** 7.6%.
- **AS 4000 and AS 2124:** AS 4000 accounts for 3.1% and AS 2124 for 2.9%.

 Agencies that name a form may differ from those that do not, so the mix is indicative.

<script>(function(){var root=document.getElementById('mastt-aup-f07');if(!root||root.getAttribute('data-maupf07-on'))return;root.setAttribute('data-maupf07-on','1');
var RM=!root.classList.contains('maupf07-js');
var mxGen=0;
var mxZero=function(el){if(!el.hasAttribute('data-final'))el.setAttribute('data-final',el.textContent);var dec=+(el.getAttribute('data-dec')||0);el.textContent=(el.getAttribute('data-pre')||'')+(0).toFixed(dec)+(el.getAttribute('data-suf')||'');};
var mxCount=function(el,g){var to=parseFloat(el.getAttribute('data-to'));if(isNaN(to))return;var fin=el.getAttribute('data-final')||el.textContent,dec=+(el.getAttribute('data-dec')||0),pre=el.getAttribute('data-pre')||'',suf=el.getAttribute('data-suf')||'',f=new Intl.NumberFormat('en-US',{minimumFractionDigits:dec,maximumFractionDigits:dec}),t0=null,d=950;el.setAttribute('data-final',fin);var step=function(t){if(g!==mxGen)return;if(t0===null)t0=t;var p=Math.min(1,(t-t0)/d),e=1-Math.pow(1-p,3);el.textContent=pre+f.format(to*e)+suf;if(p<1)requestAnimationFrame(step);else el.textContent=fin;};requestAnimationFrame(step);};
var mxIn=function(){if(root.classList.contains('maupf07-in'))return;mxGen++;var g=mxGen;root.classList.add('maupf07-in');root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(function(el){mxCount(el,g);});};
var mxOut=function(){if(!root.classList.contains('maupf07-in'))return;mxGen++;root.classList.remove('maupf07-in');root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(mxZero);};
if(RM||!('IntersectionObserver' in window)){root.classList.add('maupf07-in');}else{root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(mxZero);var io=new IntersectionObserver(function(es){es.forEach(function(e){if(e.isIntersecting&&e.intersectionRatio>=.15){mxIn();}else if(!e.isIntersecting){mxOut();}});},{threshold:[0,.15]});io.observe(root);}
root.querySelectorAll('.maupf07-tabs').forEach(function(box){var tabs=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.maupf07-tablist>.maupf07-tab')),panels=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.maupf07-panel'));var pick=function(v,focus){tabs.forEach(function(t){var on=t.getAttribute('data-v')===v;t.setAttribute('aria-selected',on?'true':'false');t.tabIndex=on?0:-1;if(on&&focus)t.focus();});panels.forEach(function(p){p.hidden=p.getAttribute('data-v')!==v;});box.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('maupf07-tab',{bubbles:true}));};tabs.forEach(function(t,i){t.addEventListener('click',function(){pick(t.getAttribute('data-v'));});t.addEventListener('keydown',function(e){var k=e.key,j=k==='ArrowRight'?i+1:k==='ArrowLeft'?i-1:-1;if(j<0&&k!=='ArrowLeft')return;e.preventDefault();var n=tabs[(j+tabs.length)%tabs.length];pick(n.getAttribute('data-v'),true);});});});
var mxTip=function(host){var tip=host.querySelector(':scope>.maupf07-tip');if(!tip){tip=document.createElement('div');tip.className='maupf07-tip';tip.setAttribute('aria-hidden','true');host.appendChild(tip);}return{show:function(x,y,h){tip.innerHTML=h;tip.style.left=x+'px';tip.style.top=y+'px';tip.classList.add('maupf07-on');},hide:function(){tip.classList.remove('maupf07-on');}};};
try{
function mxLayout(K,V,F,W,H){
var G=2,P=3,HD=22,out=[];
function sq(ids,x0,y0,w0,h0){
var x=x0,y=y0,w=w0,h=h0,list=ids.filter(function(i){return V[i]>0;}).sort(function(a,b){return V[b]-V[a];}),tot=0,k=0;
list.forEach(function(i){tot+=V[i];});
if(!tot||w<=0||h<=0){ids.forEach(function(i){out[i]=[x0,y0,0,0];});return;}
var sc=w*h/tot;
var worst=function(row,s){var sum=0,mx=-Infinity,mn=Infinity;row.forEach(function(a){sum+=a;if(a>mx)mx=a;if(a<mn)mn=a;});return Math.max(s*s*mx/(sum*sum),sum*sum/(s*s*mn));};
while(k<list.length){
var s=Math.min(w,h),row=[V[list[k]]*sc],j=k+1,sum=0,m;
while(j<list.length){var nx=row.concat(V[list[j]]*sc);if(worst(nx,s)>worst(row,s))break;row.push(V[list[j]]*sc);j++;}
row.forEach(function(a){sum+=a;});
if(w>=h){var cw=sum/h,cy=y;for(m=k;m<j;m++){var th=V[list[m]]*sc/cw;out[list[m]]=[x,cy,cw,th];cy+=th;}x+=cw;w-=cw;}
else{var rh=sum/w,cx=x;for(m=k;m<j;m++){var tw=V[list[m]]*sc/rh;out[list[m]]=[cx,y,tw,rh];cx+=tw;}y+=rh;h-=rh;}
k=j;}
ids.forEach(function(i){if(!out[i]||list.indexOf(i)<0)out[i]=[x0,y0,0,0];});}
function nest(i,r,top,lv){
var ks=K[i];if(!ks.length||lv>2)return;
var room=r;
if(!top){var hd=r[3]>48&&r[2]>64?HD:P;room=[r[0]+P,r[1]+hd,Math.max(0,r[2]-P*2),Math.max(0,r[3]-hd-P)];}
sq(ks,room[0],room[1],room[2],room[3]);
ks.forEach(function(c){var a=out[c],g=[a[0]+G/2,a[1]+G/2,Math.max(0,a[2]-G),Math.max(0,a[3]-G)];out[c]=g;nest(c,g,false,lv+1);});}
nest(F,[0,0,W,H],true,1);
return out;}
root.querySelectorAll('.maupf07-tm').forEach(function(c){var st=c.querySelector('.maupf07-tm-stage');if(!st||!st.hasAttribute('tabindex'))return;
var els=[].slice.call(st.querySelectorAll('.maupf07-tm-t')),n=els.length,K=[],V=[],P=[],i,F=n,act=-1,cur=[],drawn=[],W=0,H=+st.getAttribute('data-h'),tp=mxTip(st),cl=c.querySelector('.maupf07-tm-cl'),tot=c.querySelector('.maupf07-tm-tot'),live=c.querySelector('.maupf07-tm-live'),cLab=c.getAttribute('data-cl'),pc=new Intl.NumberFormat('en-US',{style:'percent',maximumFractionDigits:1});
for(i=0;i<=n;i++)K.push([]);
els.forEach(function(el,i){var p=el.hasAttribute('data-p')?+el.getAttribute('data-p'):n;P[i]=p;V[i]=+el.getAttribute('data-v');K[p].push(i);});
V[n]=+st.getAttribute('data-v');P[n]=-1;
var X=function(s){return String(s).replace(/&/g,'&amp;').replace(/</g,'&lt;').replace(/>/g,'&gt;').replace(/"/g,'&quot;');};
var dp=function(i){var d=0;while(P[i]>=0){i=P[i];d++;}return d;};
var un=function(i,a){for(;i>=0;i=P[i])if(i===a)return true;return false;};
var lab=function(i){return i===n?c.getAttribute('data-rl'):els[i].firstChild.textContent;};
var vtx=function(i){return i===n?c.getAttribute('data-rv'):els[i].lastChild.textContent;};
var lvl=function(i){return dp(i)-dp(F);};
var vis=function(i){var l=lvl(i);return l>=1&&l<=2&&un(i,F)&&cur[i]&&cur[i][2]>=.5&&cur[i][3]>=.5;};
var top=function(i){var a=i;while(a>=0&&P[a]!==F)a=P[a];return a>=0&&a!==n?a:-1;};
var carry=function(r,f,t){var sx=t[2]/(f[2]||1),sy=t[3]/(f[3]||1);return[t[0]+(r[0]-f[0])*sx,t[1]+(r[1]-f[1])*sy,r[2]*sx,r[3]*sy];};
var room=function(r,el,lv){if(lv==='1'&&el.hasAttribute('data-br'))return r[3]>48&&r[2]>64?'n':'0';return r[2]>64&&r[3]>38?(r[3]>54&&r[2]-18>=el.lastChild.textContent.length*(W<520?7.4:8.4)?'f':'n'):'0';};
var put=function(i,r,l){var el=els[i],s=el.style,lv=l!=null?l:el.getAttribute('data-m').charAt(0);s.left=r[0].toFixed(2)+'px';s.top=r[1].toFixed(2)+'px';s.width=r[2].toFixed(2)+'px';s.height=r[3].toFixed(2)+'px';el.setAttribute('data-m',lv+room(r,el,String(lv)));};
var lay=function(){W=st.clientWidth;if(!W)return false;cur=mxLayout(K,V,F,W,H);return true;};

## Step 19: Text block 9

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

**What this means for project teams:** Track direct approaches as well as open tenders, because a third of mid-size works went to limited tender. Templates built around AS 4000 alone will not match much of the Queensland government work that states a form, and blank method and form fields make competition impossible to measure.

 
## How much do state projects grow in cost and slip?

 State budgets list the estimated total cost of each capital project year after year. Mastt matched projects across budgets by name and region and compared the first and last estimate.

 The data covers two states:

 - **Queensland:** 12 budgets from 2014-15 to 2026-27.
- **Victoria:** Three machine-readable budget years (2016-17, 2017-18 and 2026-27), which also carry completion dates.

---

## Step 21: Text block 10

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

### Queensland estimates grow over time

 In Queensland, projects listed in three or more budgets grew a median 24.2% from first to last estimate (Figure 8). Behind that median:

 - **Grew by more than 10%:** 58.7% of those projects.
- **Fell:** 14.6% of those projects.
- **Two or more budgets:** Across all projects listed in two or more budgets, 38.1% grew by more than 10%.
- **Projects over $100 million:** They drifted least, with 24.0% growing by more than 10%.

 
### Victorian finish dates slip

 In Victoria, 27.0% of projects in two or more budgets grew by more than 10%. Of 293 projects with finish dates in more than one budget:

 - **Pushed back:** 47.1% pushed their finish date back.
- **Brought forward:** 16 brought it forward.
- **Held:** The rest held it.
- **Typical slip:** 4 quarters, and at least a quarter of those that slipped moved by 8 quarters or more.

## Step 23: Text block 11

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

### The two states are not directly comparable

 Three differences matter:

 - **Years covered:** Queensland covers more years.
- **Finish dates:** Victoria's tables list finish dates and Queensland's do not.
- **Scope:** Both estimates include scope changes as well as price growth.

 The closest like-for-like comparison, projects in two or more budgets, is 38.1% for Queensland and 27.0% for Victoria.

 
### Earlier work found systematic overruns

 The pattern is consistent with earlier work. [Terrill, Coates and Danks](https://grattan.edu.au/wp-content/uploads/2016/11/ATRF2016-cost-overruns-final-submission_updated.pdf) of the Grattan Institute analysed a sample of 836 road and rail projects over $20 million between 2001 and 2015 and found systematic cost overruns, with overruns more likely on projects that were not approved or abandoned quickly.

 That points the same way as the Queensland result, where projects that stay longer in the budget show larger estimate growth.

 **What this means for project teams:** Treat the first published estimate as a starting point, not a final figure. Report the original budget and finish date beside the current figures so drift stays visible, and compare projects of the same age rather than the same size.

 
## How often does Brisbane pick the cheapest tender?

 Brisbane City Council publishes its contract decisions. Mastt analysed 702 of them from August 2020 to April 2025:

 - **Tendered:** 618 were tendered.
- **Without tender:** 84 were awarded without tender.
- **Priced tenders:** In 467 of the tenders, the prices of the other tenderers are recorded, so the winning price can be compared.

 
### The cheapest tender does not always win

 In 40.5% of those 467 tenders (189), the contract went to someone other than the cheapest tenderer (Figure 9). That is consistent with council policy. The [Brisbane City Council Procurement Policy and Plan 2025-26](https://www.brisbane.qld.gov.au/content/dam/brisbanecitycouncil/corpwebsite/business/documents/sp103-procurement-policy-and-plan-2025-26.pdf.coredownload.pdf) includes these settings:

 - **Public tenders:** Required at $500,000 and above.
- **Local benefit:** A 30% local benefit weighting applies in the value for money assessment.
- **Value for money index:** The policy describes an index that weighs price and non-price factors equally.

 Earlier years of the period may have run under different settings.

---

## Step 25: Text block 12

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

### The race is usually close

 Among the 189 tenders not won by the cheapest tenderer, the winner was:

 - **Less than 10% dearer:** In 76 tenders.
- **10% to 25% dearer:** In 43 tenders.
- **25% or more dearer:** In 70 tenders.

 On works contracts, the cheapest of the other tenders was typically 3.8% above the winning price. The extra price paid sits in a minority of awards. Where the cheapest tender did not win, the winning price was a median 15.2% above it.

 
### Single bids and awards without tender

 Of the 618 tendered awards, 23.3% recorded a single tenderer. The rate varied by type of work:

 - **Services and goods:** 40.7% (91 awards).
- **Works:** 16.1% (347 awards).

 A typical works tender drew 3 tenderers, and half drew between 2 and 5. A single-tenderer award cannot test its price against alternatives.

 A further 84 of the 702 decisions (12.0%) were awarded without tender under procurement policy exemptions. The policy allows:

 - **Extensions:** Contract extensions by variation without a tender for routine contracts, up to $500,000 in total.
- **Sole sourcing:** Where it is in the public interest or where there is a lack of available tenderers.

 
### Lump sum or schedule of rates

 Of 597 tendered awards that state a pricing basis, 55.8% were lump sum and 44.2% schedule of rates. For works, 238 were lump sum and 103 schedule of rates (Figure 10). The two bases carry risk differently:

 - **Lump sum:** Places more price risk on the contractor.
- **Schedule of rates:** Suits work where quantities are not known up front and leaves more measurement to settle during delivery.

<script>(function(){var root=document.getElementById('mastt-aup-f10');if(!root||root.getAttribute('data-maupf10-on'))return;root.setAttribute('data-maupf10-on','1');
var RM=!root.classList.contains('maupf10-js');
var mxGen=0;
var mxZero=function(el){if(!el.hasAttribute('data-final'))el.setAttribute('data-final',el.textContent);var dec=+(el.getAttribute('data-dec')||0);el.textContent=(el.getAttribute('data-pre')||'')+(0).toFixed(dec)+(el.getAttribute('data-suf')||'');};
var mxCount=function(el,g){var to=parseFloat(el.getAttribute('data-to'));if(isNaN(to))return;var fin=el.getAttribute('data-final')||el.textContent,dec=+(el.getAttribute('data-dec')||0),pre=el.getAttribute('data-pre')||'',suf=el.getAttribute('data-suf')||'',f=new Intl.NumberFormat('en-US',{minimumFractionDigits:dec,maximumFractionDigits:dec}),t0=null,d=950;el.setAttribute('data-final',fin);var step=function(t){if(g!==mxGen)return;if(t0===null)t0=t;var p=Math.min(1,(t-t0)/d),e=1-Math.pow(1-p,3);el.textContent=pre+f.format(to*e)+suf;if(p<1)requestAnimationFrame(step);else el.textContent=fin;};requestAnimationFrame(step);};
var mxIn=function(){if(root.classList.contains('maupf10-in'))return;mxGen++;var g=mxGen;root.classList.add('maupf10-in');root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(function(el){mxCount(el,g);});};
var mxOut=function(){if(!root.classList.contains('maupf10-in'))return;mxGen++;root.classList.remove('maupf10-in');root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(mxZero);};
if(RM||!('IntersectionObserver' in window)){root.classList.add('maupf10-in');}else{root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(mxZero);var io=new IntersectionObserver(function(es){es.forEach(function(e){if(e.isIntersecting&&e.intersectionRatio>=.15){mxIn();}else if(!e.isIntersecting){mxOut();}});},{threshold:[0,.15]});io.observe(root);}
root.querySelectorAll('.maupf10-tabs').forEach(function(box){var tabs=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.maupf10-tablist>.maupf10-tab')),panels=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.maupf10-panel'));var pick=function(v,focus){tabs.forEach(function(t){var on=t.getAttribute('data-v')===v;t.setAttribute('aria-selected',on?'true':'false');t.tabIndex=on?0:-1;if(on&&focus)t.focus();});panels.forEach(function(p){p.hidden=p.getAttribute('data-v')!==v;});box.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('maupf10-tab',{bubbles:true}));};tabs.forEach(function(t,i){t.addEventListener('click',function(){pick(t.getAttribute('data-v'));});t.addEventListener('keydown',function(e){var k=e.key,j=k==='ArrowRight'?i+1:k==='ArrowLeft'?i-1:-1;if(j<0&&k!=='ArrowLeft')return;e.preventDefault();var n=tabs[(j+tabs.length)%tabs.length];pick(n.getAttribute('data-v'),true);});});});
var mxTip=function(host){var tip=host.querySelector(':scope>.maupf10-tip');if(!tip){tip=document.createElement('div');tip.className='maupf10-tip';tip.setAttribute('aria-hidden','true');host.appendChild(tip);}return{show:function(x,y,h){tip.innerHTML=h;tip.style.left=x+'px';tip.style.top=y+'px';tip.classList.add('maupf10-on');},hide:function(){tip.classList.remove('maupf10-on');}};};
try{
(function(){
var clamp=function(v,lo,hi){return Math.min(hi,Math.max(lo,v));};
var vfocus=function(el){try{return el.matches(':focus-visible');}catch(e){return true;}};
var GAP=3;
root.querySelectorAll('.maupf10-wf').forEach(function(box){
var W=JSON.parse(box.getAttribute('data-w')),R=W.r,C=W.c,N=R*C,grid=box.querySelector('.maupf10-wf-grid');if(!grid)return;
var groups=[].slice.call(grid.querySelectorAll('.maupf10-g')),cells=[].slice.call(grid.querySelectorAll('i')),items=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll('.maupf10-wf-item')),live=box.querySelector('.maupf10-wf-live'),tip=mxTip(box),tipEl=box.querySelector(':scope>.maupf10-tip');
var owner=[],blocks=[],at=0;W.n.forEach(function(k,i){if(k>0)blocks.push({start:at,size:k});for(var j=0;j<k;j++)owner.push(i);at+=k;});
var filled=owner.length,ptr=-1,pre=-1,pin=W.p,last=-1;
if(!RM){cells.forEach(function(el,s){el.style.setProperty('--d',(s/N*.32).toFixed(3)+'s');});}
var esc=function(t){return t.replace(/&/g,'&amp;').replace(/</g,'&lt;');};
var show=function(){var i=owner[ptr],cr=cells[ptr].getBoundingClientRect(),hb=box.getBoundingClientRect();
tip.show(0,0,'<span class="maupf10-wf-tc"><span class="maupf10-wf-tr"><span class="maupf10-wf-ts '+W.H[i]+'" style="--cell:'+W.C[i]+'"></span><b>'+esc(W.L[i])+'</b><span class="maupf10-wf-tv">'+W.S[i]+'</span></span><span class="maupf10-wf-tm">'+esc(W.M[i])+'</span></span>');

tipEl.style.left=clamp(cx,tw/2,Math.max(tw/2,hb.width-tw/2))+'px';tipEl.style.top=(cy-th-10<0?cr.bottom-hb.top+th+20:cy)+'px';};
var update=function(){var k=ptr>=0?owner[ptr]:pre>=0?pre:pin;
grid.classList.toggle('maupf10-act',k>=0);
groups.forEach(function(g,i){g.classList.toggle('maupf10-on',i===k);});
items.forEach(function(b,i){b.classList.toggle('maupf10-on',i===k);b.classList.toggle('maupf10-dim',k>=0&&i!==k);b.setAttribute('aria-pressed',String(pin===i));});

if(ptr>=0){show();live.textContent=W.L[owner[ptr]]+', '+W.S[owner[ptr]]+', '+W.M[owner[ptr]];}else tip.hide();};
var slotAt=function(e){var b=grid.getBoundingClientRect(),cell=(b.width-GAP*(C-1))/C;if(!b.width)return-1;var col=clamp(Math.floor((e.clientX-b.left)/(cell+GAP)),0,C-1),row=clamp(Math.floor((e.clientY-b.top)/(cell+GAP)),0,R-1);return col*R+(R-1-row);};
var set=function(s){ptr=s>=0&&s<filled?s:-1;update();};
grid.addEventListener('pointermove',function(e){if(e.pointerType==='mouse'||e.buttons)set(slotAt(e));});
grid.addEventListener('pointerdown',function(e){if(e.pointerType!=='mouse'&&grid.setPointerCapture)try{grid.setPointerCapture(e.pointerId);}catch(x){}set(slotAt(e));});
grid.addEventListener('pointerup',function(e){if(e.pointerType!=='mouse')set(-1);});
grid.addEventListener('pointercancel',function(){set(-1);});
grid.addEventListener('pointerleave',function(e){if(e.pointerType==='mouse')set(-1);});
grid.addEventListener('blur',function(){set(-1);});
grid.addEventListener('focus',function(){if(vfocus(grid)&&ptr<0)set(0);});
grid.addEventListener('keydown',function(e){if(e.key==='Escape'&&ptr>=0){e.preventDefault();set(-1);return;}
var a=ptr>=0?ptr:0,col=Math.floor(a/R),row=a%R,b=0;blocks.forEach(function(x,i){if(a>=x.start&&a<x.start+x.size)b=i;});
var mv={ArrowUp:function(){return col*R+Math.min(R-1,row+1);},ArrowDown:function(){return col*R+Math.max(0,row-1);},ArrowRight:function(){return Math.min(C-1,col+1)*R+row;},ArrowLeft:function(){return Math.max(0,col-1)*R+row;},PageDown:function(){return blocks[Math.min(blocks.length-1,b+1)].start;},PageUp:function(){return a>blocks[b].start?blocks[b].start:blocks[Math.max(0,b-1)].start;},Home:function(){return 0;},End:function(){return filled-1;}}[e.key];
if(!mv)return;e.preventDefault();set(clamp(ptr>=0?mv():0,0,Math.max(0,filled-1)));});
var ul=box.querySelector('.maupf10-wf-legend');
if(ul){ul.addEventListener('pointerleave',function(){if(pre>=0){pre=-1;update();}});
items.forEach(function(b,i){b.addEventListener('click',function(){pin=pin===i?-1:i;update();});
b.addEventListener('pointerenter',function(e){if(e.pointerType==='mouse'){pre=i;update();}});
b.addEventListener('focus',function(){if(vfocus(b)){pre=i;update();}});
b.addEventListener('blur',function(){pre=-1;update();});});}
});
})();
}catch(e){}
})();</script>

## Step 27: Text block 13

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

**What this means for project teams:** Price alone does not win council work in Brisbane, so read the evaluation weightings before you price. Check whether a tender is lump sum or schedule of rates, because it decides who carries quantity risk, and expect far more single bids on services and goods than on works.

 
## How fast does the ACT pay construction invoices?

 Mastt analysed 13,262 published ACT Government invoices of $25,000 or more to construction suppliers, worth A$4.79 billion, received from April 2017 and paid by August 2026. Each record carries the date the invoice was received and the date it was paid.

 
### Half of invoices are paid within a week

 The median invoice was paid in 7 calendar days, which is 5 business days. Half were paid within a week. The slower invoices:

 - **Over 14 days:** 18% took longer.
- **Over 20 days:** 9.8% took longer.
- **Over 30 days:** 3.4% took longer.

 The yearly median ranged from 6 days in 2022 to 11 in 2017 and 2019.

 
### Invoice size makes little difference

 Invoice size made no difference to the middle of the pack (Figure 11):

 - **Median:** 7 days for invoices under $100,000, from $100,000 to $1 million and over $1 million.
- **75th percentile:** 13, 13 and 12 days across the same three groups.

Each ridge is drawn from 200 evenly spaced percentile points of days to pay in that group. The axis stops at 40 days.</p></div>
<script>(function(){var root=document.getElementById('mastt-aup-f11');if(!root||root.getAttribute('data-maupf11-on'))return;root.setAttribute('data-maupf11-on','1');
var RM=!root.classList.contains('maupf11-js');
var mxGen=0;
var mxZero=function(el){if(!el.hasAttribute('data-final'))el.setAttribute('data-final',el.textContent);var dec=+(el.getAttribute('data-dec')||0);el.textContent=(el.getAttribute('data-pre')||'')+(0).toFixed(dec)+(el.getAttribute('data-suf')||'');};
var mxCount=function(el,g){var to=parseFloat(el.getAttribute('data-to'));if(isNaN(to))return;var fin=el.getAttribute('data-final')||el.textContent,dec=+(el.getAttribute('data-dec')||0),pre=el.getAttribute('data-pre')||'',suf=el.getAttribute('data-suf')||'',f=new Intl.NumberFormat('en-US',{minimumFractionDigits:dec,maximumFractionDigits:dec}),t0=null,d=950;el.setAttribute('data-final',fin);var step=function(t){if(g!==mxGen)return;if(t0===null)t0=t;var p=Math.min(1,(t-t0)/d),e=1-Math.pow(1-p,3);el.textContent=pre+f.format(to*e)+suf;if(p<1)requestAnimationFrame(step);else el.textContent=fin;};requestAnimationFrame(step);};
var mxIn=function(){if(root.classList.contains('maupf11-in'))return;mxGen++;var g=mxGen;root.classList.add('maupf11-in');root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(function(el){mxCount(el,g);});};
var mxOut=function(){if(!root.classList.contains('maupf11-in'))return;mxGen++;root.classList.remove('maupf11-in');root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(mxZero);};
if(RM||!('IntersectionObserver' in window)){root.classList.add('maupf11-in');}else{root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(mxZero);var io=new IntersectionObserver(function(es){es.forEach(function(e){if(e.isIntersecting&&e.intersectionRatio>=.15){mxIn();}else if(!e.isIntersecting){mxOut();}});},{threshold:[0,.15]});io.observe(root);}
root.querySelectorAll('.maupf11-tabs').forEach(function(box){var tabs=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.maupf11-tablist>.maupf11-tab')),panels=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.maupf11-panel'));var pick=function(v,focus){tabs.forEach(function(t){var on=t.getAttribute('data-v')===v;t.setAttribute('aria-selected',on?'true':'false');t.tabIndex=on?0:-1;if(on&&focus)t.focus();});panels.forEach(function(p){p.hidden=p.getAttribute('data-v')!==v;});box.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('maupf11-tab',{bubbles:true}));};tabs.forEach(function(t,i){t.addEventListener('click',function(){pick(t.getAttribute('data-v'));});t.addEventListener('keydown',function(e){var k=e.key,j=k==='ArrowRight'?i+1:k==='ArrowLeft'?i-1:-1;if(j<0&&k!=='ArrowLeft')return;e.preventDefault();var n=tabs[(j+tabs.length)%tabs.length];pick(n.getAttribute('data-v'),true);});});});
var mxTip=function(host){var tip=host.querySelector(':scope>.maupf11-tip');if(!tip){tip=document.createElement('div');tip.className='maupf11-tip';tip.setAttribute('aria-hidden','true');host.appendChild(tip);}return{show:function(x,y,h){tip.innerHTML=h;tip.style.left=x+'px';tip.style.top=y+'px';tip.classList.add('maupf11-on');},hide:function(){tip.classList.remove('maupf11-on');}};};
try{
root.querySelectorAll('.maupf11-rg').forEach(function(c){var plot=c.querySelector('.maupf11-rg-plot');if(!plot||!plot.hasAttribute('tabindex'))return;
var svg=plot.querySelector('svg'),gs=[].slice.call(plot.querySelectorAll('.maupf11-rg-r')),labs=[].slice.call(plot.querySelectorAll('.maupf11-rg-l')),tks=[].slice.call(plot.querySelectorAll('.maupf11-rg-tk')),lens=plot.querySelector('.maupf11-rg-lens'),cr=plot.querySelector('.maupf11-rg-cr'),live=c.querySelector('.maupf11-rg-live');
var A=function(k){return +c.getAttribute(k);},lo=A('data-lo'),hi=A('data-hi'),top=A('data-top'),rh=A('data-rh'),HT=A('data-h'),AX=28,VB=95,gl=(c.getAttribute('data-gl')||'').split('|'),n=gs.length,step=1,cur=-1,tp=mxTip(plot);
var X=function(s){return String(s).replace(/&/g,'&amp;').replace(/</g,'&lt;').replace(/>/g,'&gt;').replace(/"/g,'&quot;');};
var nice=function(span,count){var raw=span/Math.max(1,count)||1,p=Math.pow(10,Math.floor(Math.log10(raw))),u=raw/p;return(u>=5?5:u>=2?2:1)*p;};
var R=gs.map(function(g,i){var base=top+(i+1)*rh,ln=g.querySelector('.maupf11-rg-ln').getAttribute('d'),nums=ln.match(/-?\d*\.?\d+/g).map(Number),y=nums[1],h=[(base*10-y)/10],k,q=g.getAttribute('data-x').split(',').map(Number);for(k=3;k<nums.length;k+=2){y+=nums[k];h.push((base*10-y)/10);}
return{g:g,base:base,h:h,q1:q[0],md:q[1],q3:q[2],m:g.getAttribute('data-m'),mh:g.getAttribute('data-mh'),r:g.getAttribute('data-r'),e:(g.getAttribute('data-e')||'').split(',').map(Number),fill:g.querySelector('.maupf11-rg-f').getAttribute('d'),line:ln,lab:labs[i]?labs[i].textContent:''};});
var hAt=function(r,u){if(u<=0||u>=VB)return 0;var j=Math.min(VB-1,Math.floor(u));return r.h[j]+(r.h[j+1]-r.h[j])*(u-j);};
var yv=function(r,h){return Math.round((r.base-h)*10);};
var band=function(r){var u1=r.q1,u3=r.q3,d='M'+u1+' '+r.base*10+'L'+u1+' '+yv(r,hAt(r,u1)),j;for(j=0;j<r.h.length;j++){if(j>u1&&j<u3)d+='L'+j+' '+yv(r,r.h[j]);}return d+'L'+u3+' '+yv(r,hAt(r,u3))+'L'+u3+' '+r.base*10+'Z';};
var showLens=function(i){var r=R[i];lens.setAttribute('style',r.g.getAttribute('style'));lens.innerHTML='<path class="maupf11-rg-lf" d="'+r.fill+'"/><path class="maupf11-rg-lb" d="'+band(r)+'"/><line class="maupf11-rg-lm" x1="'+r.md+'" x2="'+r.md+'" y1="'+r.base*10+'" y2="'+yv(r,hAt(r,r.md))+'"/><path class="maupf11-rg-ll" d="'+r.line+'"/>';};
var geom=function(){var a=plot.getBoundingClientRect(),b=svg.getBoundingClientRect();return{l:b.left-a.left,w:b.width};};
var clamp=function(v,a,b){return Math.min(b,Math.max(a,v));};
var tipAt=function(ax,ay,w){var t=plot.querySelector(':scope>.maupf11-tip');if(!t)return;t.style.transform='none';var tw=t.offsetWidth,th=t.offsetHeight,l=ax+14,y;if(l+tw>w)l=ax-14-tw;y=ay-th-14;if(y<0)y=ay+14;t.style.left=clamp(l,0,Math.max(0,w-tw))+'px';t.style.top=clamp(y,0,Math.max(0,HT-th))+'px';};
var set=function(i,v,py){var g=geom(),r,j,ax,ay,f;
if(i!==cur){cur=i;gs.forEach(function(el,k){el.classList.toggle('maupf11-dm',i>=0&&k!==i);el.classList.toggle('maupf11-lf',k===i);});labs.forEach(function(el,k){el.classList.toggle('maupf11-on',k===i);});if(i>=0)showLens(i);lens.classList.toggle('maupf11-on',i>=0);}

## Step 29: Text block 14

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

### The busiest agency is among the slowest

 Transport Canberra and City Services, the agency with the most invoices (2,657), was also among the slowest. Its median was 11 days and 7.8% of its invoices took more than 30 days. Of the 13 agencies with 200 or more invoices, 8 had a median of 7 days or less.

 
### Payments land in runs

 Payments cluster on set days, which suggests they go out in runs (Figure 12):

 - **Tuesday:** 42.6% of payments.
- **Thursday:** 32.5%.
- **Wednesday:** 16.3%.
- **Friday:** 4.8%.
- **Monday:** 3.8%.

 Tuesday and Thursday together take 75.1% of payments. Two payments were dated on a weekend.

## Step 31: Text block 15

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

**What this means for project teams:** For ACT work, plan cash flow on 7 days for the central case and 20 days for the slow end, built around Tuesday and Thursday payment runs. A forecast that assumes payment on the day an invoice is approved can be out by several days.

 
## Are NSW building defect orders rising?

 Building Commission NSW issues stop work orders, prohibition orders, building work rectification orders and rectification orders. A published order stays listed only while it remains in force, so these counts are a floor. Mastt analysed the 137 orders listed to 28 September 2026, the earliest dated December 2021.

 
### Orders by year of issue

 By year of issue, the register shows (Figure 13):

 - **2021:** 1 order.
- **2022:** 4 orders.
- **2023:** 15 orders.
- **2024:** 41 orders.
- **2025:** 27 orders.
- **2026 to date:** 49 orders.

 Because orders drop off once they are revoked or complied with, earlier years are undercounted and the rise from 2023 is overstated. The 2026 count of 49 already exceeds every earlier full year, and 2025 sits below 2024, so the series shows orders still in force, not a clean count of orders issued.

---

## Step 35: Text block 17

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

Of the 137 orders, the laws they were issued under are:

 - **Residential apartment buildings:** 101 under the Residential Apartment Buildings (Compliance and Enforcement Powers) Act 2020.
- **Home building:** 30 under the Home Building Act 1989.
- **Design and building practitioners:** 4 under the Design and Building Practitioners Act 2020.
- **Not stated:** 2.

 Only 29 of the 110 rectification orders state a time to comply on the register page, and those give a median of 180 days.

 **What this means for project teams:** Keep defect records, rectification programmes and sign-off evidence in a form you can produce quickly. When an order sets a period to fix the work, plan on months, not weeks, since 180 days is typical where the period is stated.

 
## How much paperwork sits inside a standard contract?

 The NSW Government's [GC21 Edition 2](https://info.buy.nsw.gov.au/resources/gc21) general conditions come with a set of pro-forma letters for the principal's authorised person, which NSW describes as over 100. Mastt counted what each public suite holds:

 - **NSW GC21:** 109 letters in the sample letter suite.
- **NSW MW21:** 105 templates for smaller works, 70 from the principal and 35 from the contractor.
- **ACT design services:** 75 documents, of which 60 are clause-numbered notices, requests and instructions, including notices of variation and claim.

 
### Variations and claims take the most letters

 Mastt grouped the 109 GC21 letters by clause number (Figure 14):

 - **Variations and claims:** 20 letters, the most of any theme.
- **Time and programme:** 19 letters.
- **Set-up and instructions:** 18 letters.

 Each letter is tied to a clause and often to a time limit, so each one is a step a project team must track.

## Step 37: Text block 18

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

**What this means for project teams:** The administrative load is built into the contract. Map each letter to its trigger and deadline, keep a register of notices sent and received, and check which letters your special conditions have changed, because NSW notes that amended clauses mean the letters need review.

 
## How do Australian and US public owners compare?

 Two comparisons show how differently public owners pick winners and how much one buyer shapes national figures.

 
### Texas almost always takes the lowest bid, Brisbane often does not

 Texas DOT awarded 98.9% of 13,596 contracts, 2016 to 2024, at a price within $1 of the lowest bid. Brisbane gave 59.5% of its priced tenders to the cheapest tenderer (Figure 15). The rules suggest part of the reason:

 - **Texas DOT:** [TxDOT guidance](https://www.txdot.gov/manuals/tpd/lgp/letting_and_award/contract_award-i1004090.html) quotes the federal requirement that federal-aid contracts be awarded only on the basis of the lowest responsive bid.
- **Brisbane:** It scores value for money with a local benefit weighting.

---

## Step 39: Text block 19

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

Both owners see a similar number of bidders:

 - **Brisbane:** A typical works tender drew 3 tenderers.
- **Texas DOT:** A typical letting drew 3 bidders across all work and 4 on state construction.

 
### Defence is the biggest federal buyer in both countries

 Defence moves the national average in both countries:

 - **United States:** The Department of Defense accounts for 62.2% of 583,728 completed federal construction contracts and delivery orders, fiscal years 2008 to 2026.
- **Australia:** Defence accounts for 53.0% of 31,773 Commonwealth construction contracts.
- **US change rate:** Defense contracts changed value slightly less often than average (36.5% against 39.1%).
- **Australian amendment rate:** Defence contracts were amended more often than the rest (43.5% against 22.3%).

 The two measures are not identical, so compare direction, not size. Neither figure includes state or local spending.

## Step 41: Text block 20

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

**What this means for project teams:** Any national federal benchmark is mostly a Defence benchmark, in both countries, so split Defence out before you compare your own contracts. When you compare owners, check first whether the winner is chosen on price alone or on a weighted score.

<div class="maupfaq-acc"><div class="maupfaq-acc-item"><h3 class="maupfaq-acc-h"><button type="button" class="maupfaq-acc-btn" id="mastt-aup-faq-faqb46" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="mastt-aup-faq-faqp47"><span class="maupfaq-acc-tw"><span class="maupfaq-acc-title">How many Commonwealth construction contracts are amended?</span></span><span class="maupfaq-acc-ico" aria-hidden="true"></span></button></h3><div class="maupfaq-acc-panel" id="mastt-aup-faq-faqp47" role="region" aria-labelledby="mastt-aup-faq-faqb46"><div class="maupfaq-acc-in"><div class="maupfaq-acc-body"><p>33.6% were amended at least once (10,661 of 31,773 contracts, mostly 2013 to 2026). The rate runs from 14.9% under $100,000 to 72.6% over $100 million, and amendments include option exercises and time extensions.</p></div></div></div></div><div class="maupfaq-acc-item"><h3 class="maupfaq-acc-h"><button type="button" class="maupfaq-acc-btn" id="mastt-aup-faq-faqb48" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="mastt-aup-faq-faqp49"><span class="maupfaq-acc-tw"><span class="maupfaq-acc-title">What is the Commonwealth procurement threshold for construction?</span></span><span class="maupfaq-acc-ico" aria-hidden="true"></span></button></h3><div class="maupfaq-acc-panel" id="mastt-aup-faq-faqp49" role="region" aria-labelledby="mastt-aup-faq-faqb48"><div class="maupfaq-acc-in"><div class="maupfaq-acc-body"><p>The general threshold for non-corporate Commonwealth entities rose from $80,000 to $125,000 on 17 November 2025, including GST. A separate threshold of $7.5 million applies to construction services and was left unchanged.</p></div></div></div></div><div class="maupfaq-acc-item"><h3 class="maupfaq-acc-h"><button type="button" class="maupfaq-acc-btn" id="mastt-aup-faq-faqb50" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="mastt-aup-faq-faqp51"><span class="maupfaq-acc-tw"><span class="maupfaq-acc-title">How often does Brisbane pick the cheapest tender?</span></span><span class="maupfaq-acc-ico" aria-hidden="true"></span></button></h3><div class="maupfaq-acc-panel" id="mastt-aup-faq-faqp51" role="region" aria-labelledby="mastt-aup-faq-faqb50"><div class="maupfaq-acc-in"><div class="maupfaq-acc-body"><p>The cheapest tender won 59.5% of the 467 priced tenders analysed, so 40.5% went to someone else. The winner was usually within 25% of the cheapest price.</p></div></div></div></div><div class="maupfaq-acc-item"><h3 class="maupfaq-acc-h"><button type="button" class="maupfaq-acc-btn" id="mastt-aup-faq-faqb52" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="mastt-aup-faq-faqp53"><span class="maupfaq-acc-tw"><span class="maupfaq-acc-title">How long does the ACT take to pay construction invoices?</span></span><span class="maupfaq-acc-ico" aria-hidden="true"></span></button></h3><div class="maupfaq-acc-panel" id="mastt-aup-faq-faqp53" role="region" aria-labelledby="mastt-aup-faq-faqb52"><div class="maupfaq-acc-in"><div class="maupfaq-acc-body"><p>The median was 7 calendar days, or 5 business days, across 13,262 invoices from 2017 to 2026. 9.8% took longer than 20 days and 3.4% longer than 30 days.</p></div></div></div></div><div class="maupfaq-acc-item"><h3 class="maupfaq-acc-h"><button type="button" class="maupfaq-acc-btn" id="mastt-aup-faq-faqb54" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="mastt-aup-faq-faqp55"><span class="maupfaq-acc-tw"><span class="maupfaq-acc-title">How much do Queensland capital projects grow in cost?</span></span><span class="maupfaq-acc-ico" aria-hidden="true"></span></button></h3><div class="maupfaq-acc-panel" id="mastt-aup-faq-faqp55" role="region" aria-labelledby="mastt-aup-faq-faqb54"><div class="maupfaq-acc-in"><div class="maupfaq-acc-body"><p>Projects listed in three or more state budgets grew a median 24.2% from first to last estimate, and 58.7% grew by more than 10%. Across projects in two or more budgets, 38.1% grew by more than 10%.</p></div></div></div></div><div class="maupfaq-acc-item"><h3 class="maupfaq-acc-h"><button type="button" class="maupfaq-acc-btn" id="mastt-aup-faq-faqb56" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="mastt-aup-faq-faqp57"><span class="maupfaq-acc-tw"><span class="maupfaq-acc-title">How many standard letters does NSW GC21 include?</span></span><span class="maupfaq-acc-ico" aria-hidden="true"></span></button></h3><div class="maupfaq-acc-panel" id="mastt-aup-faq-faqp57" role="region" aria-labelledby="mastt-aup-faq-faqb56"><div class="maupfaq-acc-in"><div class="maupfaq-acc-body"><p>109 sample letters sit in the GC21 Edition 2 suite, which NSW describes as over 100. The MW21 contract for smaller works has 105 templates.</p></div></div></div></div><div class="maupfaq-acc-item"><h3 class="maupfaq-acc-h"><button type="button" class="maupfaq-acc-btn" id="mastt-aup-faq-faqb58" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="mastt-aup-faq-faqp59"><span class="maupfaq-acc-tw"><span class="maupfaq-acc-title">Are NSW building defect orders rising?</span></span><span class="maupfaq-acc-ico" aria-hidden="true"></span></button></h3><div class="maupfaq-acc-panel" id="mastt-aup-faq-faqp59" role="region" aria-labelledby="mastt-aup-faq-faqb58"><div class="maupfaq-acc-in"><div class="maupfaq-acc-body"><p>49 orders issued in 2026 to 28 September are listed, against 15 from 2023. The register lists only orders still in force, so earlier years are undercounted and the rise is overstated.</p></div></div></div></div></div>

var mxOut=function(){if(!root.classList.contains('maupfaq-in'))return;mxGen++;root.classList.remove('maupfaq-in');root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(mxZero);};
if(RM||!('IntersectionObserver' in window)){root.classList.add('maupfaq-in');}else{root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(mxZero);var io=new IntersectionObserver(function(es){es.forEach(function(e){if(e.isIntersecting&&e.intersectionRatio>=.15){mxIn();}else if(!e.isIntersecting){mxOut();}});},{threshold:[0,.15]});io.observe(root);}
root.querySelectorAll('.maupfaq-tabs').forEach(function(box){var tabs=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.maupfaq-tablist>.maupfaq-tab')),panels=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.maupfaq-panel'));var pick=function(v,focus){tabs.forEach(function(t){var on=t.getAttribute('data-v')===v;t.setAttribute('aria-selected',on?'true':'false');t.tabIndex=on?0:-1;if(on&&focus)t.focus();});panels.forEach(function(p){p.hidden=p.getAttribute('data-v')!==v;});box.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('maupfaq-tab',{bubbles:true}));};tabs.forEach(function(t,i){t.addEventListener('click',function(){pick(t.getAttribute('data-v'));});t.addEventListener('keydown',function(e){var k=e.key,j=k==='ArrowRight'?i+1:k==='ArrowLeft'?i-1:-1;if(j<0&&k!=='ArrowLeft')return;e.preventDefault();var n=tabs[(j+tabs.length)%tabs.length];pick(n.getAttribute('data-v'),true);});});});
var mxTip=function(host){var tip=host.querySelector(':scope>.maupfaq-tip');if(!tip){tip=document.createElement('div');tip.className='maupfaq-tip';tip.setAttribute('aria-hidden','true');host.appendChild(tip);}return{show:function(x,y,h){tip.innerHTML=h;tip.style.left=x+'px';tip.style.top=y+'px';tip.classList.add('maupfaq-on');},hide:function(){tip.classList.remove('maupfaq-on');}};};
try{
root.querySelectorAll('.maupfaq-acc').forEach(function(acc){var items=[].slice.call(acc.querySelectorAll(':scope>.maupfaq-acc-item'));items.forEach(function(it){var b=it.querySelector('.maupfaq-acc-btn');b.addEventListener('click',function(){var open=!it.classList.contains('maupfaq-open');items.forEach(function(o){o.classList.remove('maupfaq-open');o.querySelector('.maupfaq-acc-btn').setAttribute('aria-expanded','false');});if(open){it.classList.add('maupfaq-open');b.setAttribute('aria-expanded','true');}});});});
}catch(e){}
})();</script>
</div>

## Step 43: Text block 21

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

## Sources and methodology

 **Records.** This is an internal Mastt study of published public records. It draws on:

 - About 31,800 Commonwealth contract notices for building, construction and related services (mostly 2013 to 2026).
- About 68,000 Queensland contract disclosure rows (mostly 2019 to 2026).
- Project listings in Queensland state budgets (12 budgets from 2014-15 to 2026-27) and Victorian state budgets (three machine-readable years).
- 702 Brisbane City Council contract decisions (August 2020 to April 2025).
- 13,262 ACT invoices (2017 to 2026).
- 137 NSW building work orders (to 28 September 2026).

 We also counted standard contract letter suites and used Texas DOT bid results (2016 to 2024) and US federal contract awards (fiscal years 2008 to 2026) for comparison.

 **Filters.** These rules were applied to the records:

 - A Commonwealth contract counts as construction when it covers building and construction services or has a works-type description.
- Growth is measured from original to latest value, and changes below minus 95% or above 2,000% are excluded.
- State project growth is measured from first to last budget listing, matched by name and region, and excludes changes below minus 90% or above 1,000%.
- ACT invoices with a missing received date, or a paid date before the received date, are excluded.
- The excess near $80,000 compares the $75,000 to $79,999 band with a smooth curve fitted to $5,000 bands from $40,000 to $200,000, leaving out $75,000 to $95,000.

 **Limits.** Findings are signals, not proof:

 - Commonwealth amendments include option exercises and time extensions.
- Many Queensland records leave the method or form blank.
- State budget estimates include scope changes.
- The NSW register lists only orders in force.
- Texas figures describe Texas DOT work only.
- No contractor or company names are used.

 Includes public sector information from Australian federal, state, territory and local governments, used under Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 licences.

4. Queensland Government. [Contract disclosure guide](https://www.forgov.qld.gov.au/__data/assets/pdf_file/0021/183630/Contract-disclosure-guide.pdf)
